STATUS=`READY_FOR_SELF_REVIEW`. Both layers built: scan-based pre-clean + teardown in `RealHoleTerrainTests` / `BakedPivotRegressionTests`, and `Assets/Scripts/Editor/SceneHygiene/StagedHoleSceneGuard.cs`. The predicate and sweep were promoted to `public static` on `CaptureSceneSetup` so there is **one** implementation, not four; `Golfin.Gameplay.Tests.asmdef` gained a single `Golfin.Physics.Viewer` reference to reach it. **Verified:** two back-to-back full EditMode runs at 1111 / 1108 pass / **0 fail** / 3 pre-existing skips, each followed by a quoted `GetSceneManagerSetup()` dump showing ShellScene only; 18/18 hole sweep green with the guard live; all four authoring-protection branches correct (dirty+active survives, dirty+non-active survives, clean+active survives, clean+non-active is closed); `Hole_06_Geo.unity` byte-identical sha256 after being dirtied and swept four times; `LastSceneManagerSetup.txt` = ShellScene only; **zero `.unity` diffs**. Three failures seen in earlier runs were attributed to the tree, not the task, by control runs with every change stashed. **iter-1 passed all 11 acceptance items but the RED-TEAM GATE ESCALATED IT, and was right** — it reproduced Cesar's exact symptom (`ShellScene + Hole_06_Geo` left after a clean all-green run). **The real cause is a THIRD vector no version of this task had ever named:** `PhysicsLabAutoRestore` (`Assets/Scripts/Editor/Physics/PhysicsLabHolePicker.cs`) auto-loads EditorPref `Golfin.PhysicsLab.CurrentHole` (= **6**) on *any* LabScaffold open — including the additive open `ActionButtonRenderingTests` does — and never re-validated after its `delayCall` deferral, so it was observed injecting `Hole_06_Geo` next to ShellScene with no LabScaffold open at all (`Editor.log:64820-64826`). **That pref is why the leftover was always hole 06 and never a random hole from the sweep.** iter-2 (2026-08-11, scope widened on Cesar's call) fixes it at source (restore only on `OpenSceneMode.Single`; re-validate after the deferral), narrows the guard's condition (d) to **ShellScene only** so it stops fighting the physics-lab workflow, and replaces the guard's editor-load `delayCall` — which RACES Unity's post-`[InitializeOnLoad]` scene restore and was caught LOSING that race, letting a leak survive a full kill/relaunch — with an idle-settle pump. iter-2 verified: guard protects the lab shape (closed=0) and sweeps the leak shape (closed=1); two back-to-back suites at 1116 with clean dumps and zero auto-restores; kill/relaunch swept at `hook=editor-load`; zero `.unity` diffs. Earlier iter-1 evidence (2026-08-11): the launcher pass ran the real `GOLFIN/Capture 1v1/Record Versus Launch` round trip and came back to ShellScene only with `CaptureSceneSetup`'s snapshot/restore log text unchanged, and the killed-editor case did the real thing — staged `LabScaffold` + `Hole_06_Geo`, confirmed `LastSceneManagerSetup.txt` had persisted the leak, `kill -9`'d the Editor, relaunched, and Unity reopened the hole scene only for the guard to close it at load on `delayCall`. **This is the vector that made the bug survive editor restarts, and it is now self-healing.** The `kill -9`'s crash-recovery residue in the gitignored `Assets/_Recovery/` was removed.)* Cesar had `Hole_06_Geo` reintroduced into the hierarchy and left there **twice on 2026-08-10**, after both the Architect and Code had assured him K16 (`hole_scene_leftover_v2`) closed it. **The assurances were wrong because v1 and v2 both scoped the bug to the capture launchers.** The dominant vector is the **EditMode test suite**, which no version of this task ever touched — and which runs on every task's acceptance gate, i.e. far more often than any launcher. `RealHoleTerrainTests` opens all 18 `Hole_NN_Geo` scenes **additively into the LIVE editor hierarchy** (`:131`, one per `[TestCase]`), and its `[OneTimeTearDown]` (`:85-91`) closes only what is still in the plain static `s_HoleCache` (`:60`) — **any domain reload wipes that dictionary while the scenes stay open**, so the teardown iterates an empty dict, closes nothing, and reports success; a cancelled run skips the teardown entirely. Either way the staged scenes *become* the hierarchy and Unity persists them to `LastSceneManagerSetup.txt`, surviving restarts. **Evidence (Architect, from the live machine 2026-08-10):** (a) `Library/LastSceneManagerSetup.txt.bak` records the leak verbatim — ShellScene active + `Hole_06_Geo` loaded; (b) `Editor.log` last 700k lines show holes 01–18 each opened additively **exactly 2×** = the two suite runs Cesar saw, with `Hole_06` at 3× (the extra one preceded by a `LabScaffold` load = capture-launcher signature); (c) **the proof** — a sweep opens `Hole_05`→`Hole_06` at ~line 9296637 and ~280 lines later `[CaptureSceneSetup] Excluding staged hole scene from snapshot: Hole_06_Geo` fires, i.e. the hole was **still open from the test sweep** when a protected launcher later called `Capture()`; `CaptureSceneSetup` was cleaning up after a mess it did not make; (d) coverage audit — **4 of 26** hole-scene stagers call `CaptureSceneSetup` (`LoopV2SmokeBotMenu`, `VersusHudCaptureMenu`, `SmokeRunner2eMenu`, `SmokeRunner2fMenu`) — exactly K16's scope; every test fixture and every `*DemoRecorder` is unprotected. **Fix is two layers:** (1) reload-proof, scan-based pre-clean in `[OneTimeSetUp]` + scan-based `[OneTimeTearDown]` in `RealHoleTerrainTests` and `BakedPivotRegressionTests` (the pre-clean is what makes a previous aborted run self-heal); (2) an always-on editor `StagedHoleSceneGuard` that closes a `Hole_NN_Geo` **only** when it is non-active AND non-dirty AND ShellScene/LabScaffold is also open AND the editor is not playing/compiling — so deliberate hole authoring is never touched — plus a manual sweep menu item and an EditorPrefs off-switch. **Do NOT wire `TestRunnerApi`** (asmdef compile risk); `afterAssemblyReload` covers the test vector. The 18-hole sweep coverage STAYS — do not shrink the tests to dodge the problem. **The acceptance gate is deliberately harsh** (two back-to-back full suite runs each with a quoted `GetSceneManagerSetup()` dump, both directions of the authoring-protection test, the killed-editor case, a `cat` of `LastSceneManagerSetup.txt`, and zero `.unity` diffs) because this bug has now been declared fixed twice without ever being verified against the real vector. Spec + kickoff: `Docs/Specs/Active/hole_scene_leftover_v3/SPEC.md` and the `TellCode.md` pointer.

> **✅ DONE · 2026-08-10 · `auto_club_selection`** (approved by Cesar; folder moved to `Docs/Specs/Completed/`; shipped in `43d8a34c9`) — the game now auto-picks the player's club before every shot: **Driver on the tee, never the Driver off it**, and elsewhere the **shortest club in the equipped bag that still reaches the pin** (overshoot-everything → longest non-driver). Re-runs every shot, so a manual pick applies only to the shot it was made for. Implemented by the orchestrator at Cesar's direct request — **the subagent chain was not used**. New pure `AutoClubSelector` (mirrors `PutterModeSurfaceController`: primitives + POCOs in, bag index or −1 out) + `AutoSelectClubForNextShot()` on `PhysicsLabController` with three call sites (AtRest, reposition, hole start), **all placed AFTER the existing §2f block so the green rule always wins** — the selector returns −1 in putter mode, so landing on the green stays pure §2f. Selection commits through **`ClubContext.RequestSelection` + `ClubSelectionBroadcast.Raise`**, never bare `SetClub` (the Order 762 live-stat lesson); that pair is what makes the HUD club button actually track the pick. `ClubEntry` gains **`IsDriver`** (bool, not `ClubType`) — the SPEC's documented asmdef fallback, taken because `Golfin.Gameplay.UI` cannot reference Assembly-CSharp where `Golfin.Inventory.ClubType` lives; needed because **Driver and Wood both map to lab index 0**, so the lab index alone cannot express "no driver off the tee, but a wood is fine". Populated in both builders (`ClubContextPopulator`, `LabInventoryStub`). New `_autoClubSelectEnabled` toggle, default true; false returns on line 1 of both entry points. **NO scene, prefab, CSV or `Assets/Scripts/Physics/` sim edits; `VersusBot`/`BotDriver`/`BotClubSync` and the K11 gate untouched — the Driver stays manually selectable off the tee.** EditMode **1109 / 1106 pass / 0 fail / 3 pre-existing skips**, incl. 20 new `AutoClubSelectorTests`; suite membership was **proven, not assumed** (the MCP runner silently ignores class filters, so a deliberate tripwire test was used to confirm the class is in the run, then removed). Live acceptance through the REAL entry path (`GOLFIN/Smoke/Loop v2/Hole 1 Playthrough`: ShellScene → PLAY gate → Hole 1), two full playthroughs both holed out: HUD club button reads **DRIVER 250 yd on the tee**, **WOOD 230 yd** at 429 yd out (driver refused off-tee), **P. WEDGE 120 yd** at 23 yd, and the picks are arithmetically exact against Clubs.csv (250/230/180/120/30 yd). The bot calls `SetClub` before *every* stroke and each subsequent AtRest still re-picked — an adversarial proof of the re-run-every-shot rule. **Self-caught defect, fixed before delivery:** the reposition call site sat after `if (target < 0) return;`, the COMMON case, so OB/water drops would have been skipped entirely; restructured to run unconditionally at the end. **NOT verified, do not infer:** the **OB/water drop path has zero real-flow evidence** (neither playthrough went OB), the driver-still-selectable claim is a code invariant nobody re-shot, a *human* manual override was never driven by finger, and there is no on-device pass. **Two findings surfaced, not fixed (out of scope per SPEC):** (1) the **§2f `ClubContext` gap is player-visible** — on the green the world model is the putter but the HUD button still reads P. WEDGE, because §2f calls bare `SetClub`; this task makes it more conspicuous by being correct everywhere else, so it wants a follow-up Quick task. (2) **P-006 evidence:** the bot's calibration has the driver carrying ~403 m (441 yd) at power 0.96 against a `baseDistance` of 250 yd — auto-select uses `baseDistance` per SPEC and does not compensate. Folder: `Docs/Specs/Completed/auto_club_selection/`.

> **✅ FIXED · 2026-08-10 · `hole1_cup_buried_under_green`** — Hole 1's black cup disc renders again: measured `-23.12 mm` (buried) → `+6.00 mm` clearance, confirmed on a **fresh load from disk** and visually in a real-flow green-side frame (flagstick went from tapering into unbroken turf to having a distinct dark disc at its base). **The spec's own instruction — "then re-import Hole 1" — was WRONG and Cesar caught it before it ran.** `HoleGeoImporter` builds every hole with `NewScene(EmptyScene, Single)` and overwrites `Hole_NN_Geo.unity` wholesale (`:286`/`:481`), so a re-import destroys everything authored afterwards — above all the **trees** (Hole 1 carries **1362** terrain tree instances; the importer deliberately does not place them, `:468`) — and regenerates the baked sim data the multiplayer bots read (`zones.json`, `heightmap.bytes`, `tree_obstacles.csv`). **A shipped hole must be repaired surgically, never re-imported.** Fixed in two halves: (1) new editor tool `Assets/Scripts/Editor/CourseImporter/CupReseatTool.cs` — `GOLFIN > Course > Cups > Measure Cup Seating` (read-only) and `Reseat Buried Cups` (fix + save only changed scenes) — which measures each cup's top against the green's **MeshCollider** at the cup XZ and re-seats against that MEASURED surface; safe because the disc is cosmetic only (the importer destroys its collider, so it has no physics role — capture is `CupSpec`/`RealCupDetector` off the pin). The saved-scene diff was **36 lines**: the cup's `m_LocalPosition.y` plus a default `UniversalAdditionalLightData` URP auto-adds to the Directional Light on any save; trees/terrain/greens/baked data untouched. (2) `HoleGeoImporter` now seats future imports on the measured mesh too (the green's collider exists by then — mesh at `:2742`, cup at `:2840`), falls back to the old analytic `pinSeatY` if the ray misses, and **logs a warning naming the millimetres when the legacy datum would have buried the cup** — so a bad bake announces itself at import instead of hiding until someone films it. Shared constant `HoleGeoImporter.CupSurfaceClearanceM = 6 mm` referenced by the tool, so repaired and imported holes can't drift. **6 mm, not the 10–20 mm the spec proposed:** the 17 correct holes sit 1.3–6.4 mm proud, so 6 mm is inside the range already accepted visually; the load-bearing change is the **datum**, not the margin. **NOT done:** the other 17 holes have not been re-measured with the tool (open them and run the measure pass; re-run after any green re-bake), and the smoke-bot menu entry still reads "(Hole 1, cup is buried)".


> **✅ DONE · 2026-08-10 · `power_gauge_target_marker`** (Order 357, approved by Cesar off the video; folder moved to `Docs/Specs/Completed/`) — the landing target placed in map view now becomes a **white radial notch on the power gauge** at the % of club carry that would land the shot there. The player flicks toward a visible goal instead of doing mental math. **The power system is UNTOUCHED** — no `ComputePower`, overpower, `ControlsConfig` or perfect-zone change; this is a readout, not the Golf-Clash-style target-locked power that was explicitly rejected. Seam: one new `ShotController.MapTargetCarryM` (**metres**, `-1` = none) written in `MapViewController.CloseImmediate()` beside the existing yaw write-back, read by `PowerGaugeWidget`, drawn procedurally by `PowerGaugeGraphic` (a 2.5°-wide quad from `inner−4` to `outer+4`, after the fill quads so it sits on top). Metres rather than a fraction is the load-bearing choice: a club change **re-derives** the notch instead of freezing a stale percentage. Yards-mode only (putter mode forces `MarkerFrac01 = −1` — the green grid is the putter's targeting tool); pinned at 1.2 and tinted red past overpower reach; cleared at **`CommitFlick`, deliberately not `TransitionToIdle`** — a fumbled flick routes there too and must not eat the marker the player just placed. **Also closed (the SPEC's verified gap): `PowerGaugeWidget._maxCarryYards` had ZERO callers**, so the gauge's yards text read `250 × power` for every club forever; `ResolveCarryYards()` now reads `ClubContext.SelectedDistance` live (same authority as the club button and the map's landing default), with a seed added at `PhysicsLabController.ExitPutterMode`. **NO scene edits, no prefab edits, no new art** — the notch is vertices in the existing graphic, so `ShotInProgressUiGate` + the widget CanvasGroup cover show/hide for free. EditMode **1089 / 1086 pass / 0 fail / 3 pre-existing skips**, incl. 11 new `PowerGaugeMarkerTests` (11/11 individually). Live acceptance on **Hole 1 through the REAL entry path** (ShellScene → PLAY → hole card → real `HoleMap.onClick` → production `TrySetAimFromScreenPoint` → real map `SHOOT.onClick`): `marker_invariants.json` **23/23 PASS** + 6 frames at 1170×2532 — write-back exact (`_aimedCarryM` 91.75 m → `MapTargetCarryM` 91.75 m), notch at 0.4014 vs expected 0.4014, Driver 250 yd → Wood 230 yd moves it to 0.4363 with the target unchanged, shot clears it (TURN 2, clean gauge), putter mode shows none, and the yards text goes **150.0 → 138.0 yd** on the club change — the discriminating proof of the wiring fix (the Driver row can't discriminate: 250 == the old default). **NOT verified, do not infer:** flick-*to*-the-notch → ball lands on the map target (no carry-vs-marker measurement was taken), **P-006 evidence — the session fired at 60%, not 100%, so no `SelectedDistance` vs actual-carry data point exists**, and the red pinned unreachable state has EditMode math only, no live frame. The marker is exactly as truthful as `SelectedDistance`, which is what P-006 gates. New editor-only harness `Assets/Scripts/UI/Editor/PowerGaugeMarkerVerifyBot.cs` (beyond the SPEC's file list, flagged) — it also caught 6 phantom captures on the first run (`SnapPlayModeSafe` returns a path for a file it never wrote when the editor is unfocused) and refused to cite them. **VIDEO (the sign-off artifact):** `videos/power_gauge_target_marker_marker.mp4` — 1170×2532, 51.4 s, captioned; tee with a clean gauge → open the map → place the landing → SHOOT → notch appears at that % → pull to the notch → swap club (notch moves, yards text 155.0 → 142.6) → flick → next stroke markerless. Also at `Docs/Reports/Media/2026-08-10_power_gauge_target_marker.mp4`. **Two recorder lessons, both Cesar-caught or self-caught:** (1) the demo recorder was FIRST hand-rolled (its own `RecorderController`, 137 lines / 44% copied from `MapViewStrictCropDemoRecorder`) — Cesar challenged it and it was rewritten onto the sanctioned `BotVideoRecorder` (`ResetSessionGuard` → `CustomOutputPath` → `MaxRecordSecondsSessionOverride` → `ArmDeferred`/`BeginDeferred`, `End()` left to `LoopV2SmokeBotMenu`), so the engine owns Game-View pinning, Y-flip locking, `record_info.json`, the watchdog and the one-clip-per-session guard and the file supplies only the sequence + captions; no `Scenarios.cs` entry (standing ban). (2) The first cut **captioned a shot that never happened** — `TickArrow` cancels a swing after `MaxTotalPasses` (10 ≈ 8 s), the demo held one pull ~9.4 s, so `EndExternalDrag` silently no-op'd and the closing caption sat over a TURN-1 frame with the notch still drawn; fixed by short pull segments each ending in `CancelExternalDrag` plus a hard `Debug.LogError` assertion ("FLICK DID NOT COMMIT … NOT shippable") that reads `MapTargetCarryM` after release. Captions also had to be hand-wrapped to ≤26 chars/line (`build_bot_video.py` uses `fontsize = h/32` ≈ 79 px; longer lines clip off both frame edges). **Note:** a parallel session committed this work mid-flight as `9047d6444` (+ `PhysicsLabController` swept into `ec219da71`); content re-verified against HEAD after the fact.

> **✅ DONE · 2026-08-10 · `map_view_strict_crop_indicators`** (Order 355, approved by Cesar off the video; folder moved to `Docs/Specs/Completed/`) — the hole map now shows **only playable area, always**, and the flag/ball get floating edge-clamped indicators. Two files: `MapViewController.cs` (+732/−41) and `MapViewAimingTests.cs` (+453, 16 new tests). Supersedes ONLY 354c's framing target: the fit set is now **ball + landing disc**, not ball + flag — the flag left the framing set and became an *indicator* target, which is what makes strict containment achievable at all (a whole-hole frame cannot fit inside a 261 m-deep OB rect). Everything else 354 built (axis snap, env hide, zoom-out caps, OB-rect loader, `SolveShowRegionPose`) is kept and extended. **THE INVARIANT:** the camera's ground footprint (4 viewport corners ray-cast onto the ball's plane) ⊆ the OB rect, every frame, on every path — open framing, the `AnchorBallToBottom` fallback, pan and pinch — with a `#if UNITY_EDITOR` tripwire in `Update()`. Pan clamps the **footprint** per axis (slide-along-edge), not the focus point as 354 did; pinch zoom-**out** is refused dynamically when the candidate FOV would push the footprint out. One shared `SolveIndicatorPlacement` drives both flag and ball so docking is continuous by construction; procedural placeholder art, two serialized sprite slots for Robin. EditMode **1078 / 1075 pass / 0 fail / 3 pre-existing skips**. Verified through the REAL entry path (ShellScene → PLAY → hole card `ActionButton` → real `HoleMap.onClick`) on **Holes 1, 5 and 6**: footprint inside the rect on all three, **750 live pan steps across 5 directions → 0 violations** (each stop lands exactly on the rect boundary; the diagonal hits both limits = slide confirmed), zoom-out refused at containment, flag **docks on the green** on Hole 6 and after panning on Hole 1 (icon position == `WorldToScreenPoint(flag)` exactly), SHOOT closes + writes back aim, world fully restored, **0 `INVARIANT VIOLATION` lines in the whole session log**. **Three defects were caught in play mode and fixed** — all invisible to EditMode: (1) `Screen.width` reports the Game View *window* (2070×1772) not the render surface (1170×2532), so indicators were solved in the wrong space and the flag **never docked**; now uses `_mapCam.pixelWidth/Height`. (2) On Hole 5 (41.5° off axis, driver) the containment zoom threw the **ball off the right edge** at viewport x = 1.196 while the landing stayed on screen — exactly backwards; added a lateral re-seat + a Hole-5-real-rect regression test. (3) The ball seated **under the SHOOT button**; the seat window now stops short of it, and where containment makes that impossible the ball counts as *not docked* so its indicator floats clear with an arrow pointing back down at it. **Still manual/on-device:** the pinch and two-finger-pan *gestures* themselves (no `Touchscreen` in the Editor — `PanCamera` and the FOV gate were driven live, the `HandleTouchInput` plumbing to them was not), and a green-side lie visually (proven numerically over 12 carry×lie combos on Hole 1 and 9 on Hole 5). **Flagged, pre-existing, untouched:** `DoFrameReadbackAndDump("aimed")` calls `SetAimYawDirectly` → `PositionMapCamera()`, so a pan within ~1 s of opening is silently re-framed away; and water renders near-black in map view on Holes 5/6. **VIDEO (the sign-off artifact):** `videos/map_view_strict_crop_indicators.mp4` — 1170×2532, 39.3 s, captioned; open → pan → flag docks on the hole → ball indicator points back → containment stop → SHOOT → world restored, every camera move through the production `PanCamera` and every interaction through a real widget `onClick`. Produced by a NEW editor-only recorder `Assets/Scripts/UI/Editor/MapViewStrictCropDemoRecorder.cs` (modelled on the `*DemoRecorder` family) — deliberately beyond the SPEC's "single file" scope, flagged in the report and accepted. Pinch is absent from the clip on purpose: no `Touchscreen` in the Editor, so staging it would have meant the recorder duplicating the gate's own logic.

> **✅ DONE · 2026-08-10 · `aim_camera_ball_centering`** (approved by Cesar 2026-08-10 — "all tasks from yesterday and today are done"; close-out accepts the two pending Architect calls AS-IS: D2 live-widget viewport Y 0.5000 instead of the mockup's 0.4234, D3 tee clamp settling at 6.42 m on Hole 1; folder moved to `Docs/Specs/Completed/`, STATUS `DONE`) — full-swing aim framing reworked so the 3D ball projects at the SAME viewport point as the 2D `CentralBallWidget` sprite and the camera closes from 8.54 m to 3.31 m. **Single change point: `PhysicsLabController.ApplyCameraYaw` INTERNALS only** — signature unchanged (`MapViewController` reaches it by reflection on the name; verified still binding), putter branch byte-identical to HEAD (`diff` → IDENTICAL) **— true at pass 1/2 only; superseded by PASS 3 below, which centres the putt camera too**, `ChaseCamera` / `LoopCameraDirector` untouched, zero sim files touched. Levers ranked in the report (safeFrac→1.0 gives 5.80 m; nearest-marker-only; tee-only FOV bump — the only one that reaches 3 m, and out of scope). **PASS 2, same day, on Cesar's follow-up** (*"Fix botdriver as well. Camera distance is ok, but make it closer for the chase cam so the ball appears bigger then."*): (a) **`BotDriver.AimChaseCameraAtCup` no longer carries its own copy of the legacy 8/3 framing** — rather than copy the NEW math into it (which would re-create the same drift), `ApplyCameraYaw` was made `internal` and a new `internal ApplyAimCameraAt(Camera, ballPos, yaw)` seam sets the exact at-rest state and delegates, so there is now **exactly one aim-framing implementation**. `MapViewController` reaches `ApplyCameraYaw` by reflection with `Public|NonPublic|Instance` — `internal` is NonPublic to reflection, verified live that it still resolves. `grep "lookDir \* 8f"` now returns **one** hit project-wide: the putter branch. (b) **Chase cam closed 3.0/1.8 → 2.0/1.2** — a uniform ×2/3 so the framing ANGLE is unchanged (30.96° before and after) and only distance closes: camera→ball **3.499 m → 2.332 m = 1.50× larger ball in flight**, measured by driving the production `RunLateUpdateLogic` through its `FrameCamera(dt)` seam with a live target and converging 400 ticks. Scene values override C# defaults, so `LabScaffold.unity`'s serialized pair moved too; the three **lab** scenes (`PhysicsLab_Hole1/Range/Dashboard`) deliberately keep 3/1.8 to avoid the unrelated serialized-default backfill — sweep them separately if wanted. Post-pass-2 suite: **1050 / 1047 pass / 0 fail / 3 skips** — note the first run after these edits reported 2 failures (`AudioEmitterTests.MinInterval_SecondBounceWithinInterval_IsSuppressed`, `GameplaySceneLoaderTests.UnloadGameplay_RestoresBottomNav`) that an immediate no-change re-run cleared; both are timing/state-sensitive tests in subsystems this task never touches, recorded as flaky rather than quietly re-run until green. **Also still true:** `PhysicsLab_Hole1.unity` is stale (saving it backfilled 9 lines of serialized defaults from other tasks; reverted, that scene left alone), and `/usr/bin/git` on this Mac now fails with the Xcode license gate (`exit 69`) — worked around with `DEVELOPER_DIR=/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools`, real fix needs Cesar's `sudo`. Still manual: map-view close→restore, aim→chase handoff feel, depressed-lie `AdjustCameraForDepression`, on-device aspect. **PASS 3 — AFTER close-out, commit `5d938c9a8`** (*"You did not center the camera during putting."*): the SPEC had scoped putting OUT (§3/§5 legacy pose verbatim) and the ball sat ~62% down screen on a putt. `ApplyCameraYaw`'s putter branch now runs the same solver at its OWN distance/height; **`_puttCamDistanceM` / `_puttCamHeightM` default to the legacy 8 m / 3 m on purpose** — the putt view must fit the 15 m `PutterAimLine` and the green-reading grid, so this moves WHERE the ball sits on screen, not how much green is visible. Live on the Hole 1 green (`IsPutt=True`, ball 2.63 m from the cup): stand-off **8.544 m unchanged**, ball viewport **(0.5000, 0.5000)** dx/dy 0.0000, cup at (0.5000, 0.5616) directly above; pitch 12.8°→20.56° is what lifts it to centre. **The first putt test was a circular gate** — it called `SolveAimCameraPose` directly with hardcoded 8/3 and would have passed whether or not the branch changed; Cesar asking to see it is what surfaced that. Replaced with `ApplyCameraYaw_PutterBranch_CentersTheBallAtTheLegacyStandoff` (real controller + real `ShotController(IsPutt=true)` through `ApplyAimCameraAt`, asserting Unity's own `WorldToViewportPoint`) + `PutterCameraTunables_DefaultToTheLegacyStandoff`, and **proven to catch a revert**: restoring the legacy `LookAt` failed by name (viewport Y 0.382 vs 0.4234), passed again on restore. Suite 1111 / 1107 pass / 3 skips (1 intermittent in an untouched subsystem). **VIDEO:** `Docs/Specs/Completed/aim_camera_ball_centering/videos/close_camera_and_putt_centering.mp4` — 27 s, 1170×2532, captioned, flip-verified on all 1058 consecutive frame pairs; tee 6.6 m → next lie 3.3 m → chase 2.3 m → putt centred. > **DONE (Cesar-approved) 2026-08-07 · `tree_occlusion_fade`** — **cone shipped at 45°/60° (Cesar's call after the A/B video), superseding SPEC §4.1's 10/16. Window CONFIRMED rendering on bark AND leaves at the shipped default** (`videos/tree_occlude_fade_ab.mp4` phase 1; 2× crop `screenshots/occfade_shipped_45_60_crop2x.png`). EditMode **1023/1020 pass, 0 fail** at the new defaults. **The video earned its keep twice:** it exposed that after the terminal `SetTarget(null)`, `ChaseCamera.CurrentFocus` degrades to `_shotOrigin` — the origin of the shot that just *finished* — so the cone pointed BEHIND the camera exactly when the ball was sitting in trees (SPEC §5.4 had judged that fallback harmless; it is not). Fixed: `TryResolveFocus` prefers the live ball transform, `CurrentFocus` is the fallback. **`Vegetation.shader` + its `.meta` were force-added at close-out** — the whole `Assets/Packs/` vendor tree is gitignored (`.gitignore:104`) and had **0** files tracked, so without `git add -f` the shader half of the feature would have existed only on Cesar's machine. The `.meta` matters as much as the shader: it pins GUID `e80a1e91e51638b47b825fa1c86cbb65`, which all 7 retargeted `.mat` files reference — a re-import without it would mint a new GUID and break every one. **Evidence media (`screenshots/`, `videos/`) is NOT committed by design** — `.gitignore:246` / `:180` ignore those folders for every task; it lives locally in `Docs/Specs/Completed/tree_occlusion_fade/`. Still device-only per §5.10: dither grain at retina DPI + frame time. Original iter-1 detail below.
>
> **⛔ (iter-1 history) `tree_occlusion_fade`** — Genshin/BOTW dithered see-through window so trees between camera and ball stop hiding the shot. **Implementation complete; the visual is NOT yet proven.** Built: new globals-only `TreeOccludeFadeDriver` (`Assets/Scripts/Physics/Viewer/`, `beginCameraRendering` hook, cone/strength state machine, `Disabled` kill switch), an identical dither-clip injection in **5** `Vegetation.shader` passes (Forward/DepthOnly/DepthNormals/GBuffer/Universal2D — ShadowCaster + Meta deliberately untouched, verified programmatically), 7 bark/impostor `.mat` retargeted URP/Lit → Custom/Vegetation, 16 EditMode tests. Shader compiles **0 errors**; EditMode **1023/1020 pass, 0 fail**. **Verified on a real Hole 1 through the player's own entry path** (splash PLAY → PRACTICE → hole PLAY → production `ShotController` drag fire): `strength=1.000` when active, kill switch `1.000→0.000→1.000`, map view gate → `0.000`, focus tracks the live ball through flight. **Two SPEC §4.1 premises were FALSE and are corrected in code:** (1) **`Camera.main` is NOT the gameplay camera** — during a hole the MainCamera tag stays on the ShellScene camera at `(0,1,-10)`, so gating on it pinned the cone origin there and held strength at 0; resolve by the `ChaseCamera` component instead. (2) **`ChaseCamera.CurrentFocus` is `(0,0,0)` while aiming** — `LoopCameraDirector` only arms the chase camera in `ArmChaseForShot` and leaves it dormant before that, so the cone would have pointed at the world origin for the whole aiming phase; added a `LoopCameraDirector.CurrentBall` fallback. Also corrected: **Hole 6 needs zero retargeting** (its BSP firs already have bark AND leaves on Custom/Vegetation), and **Spruce is on `Shader Graphs/Bark_URP`/`Leaves_URP`, not the Mobile_Tree_Bundle NoWind shaders** (report-only per §6; no Spruce or `StandaloneTrees` exists on Hole 1 or 6 at all). **⛔ Why blocked:** the occluded scenario WAS reached in real play (ball at rest in ROUGH behind a trunk filling 2/3 of the screen, `strength=1.000`, 2 trees in cone at 40.4 m) but **the trunk does not visibly fade in that frame**, and I could not distinguish "working but too narrow" from "not executing" — the gate is *angular*, so a trunk ~1 m from the camera sits mostly outside a 16° cone even while filling the screen. The decisive widened-cone A/B did not complete (gameplay stack unloaded mid-freeze). **Next run (~15 min):** `GOLFIN > Physics > Capture Tree Occlude Fade`, then at `HOLDING frozen` capture 3 frames — shipped 10°/16°, `MaxOpacityCut=0` (an off-state needing no ramp, so it survives `timeScale=0`), and cone 45°/60°. `ReleaseHold` now holds the freeze under orchestrator control, so the race is already fixed. **⚠️ `Vegetation.shader` is in a gitignored path** (`.gitignore:104` = `Assets/Packs/`) and is untracked — close-out MUST `git add -f` it or the shader half never reaches the repo. **⚠️ Two capture-path defects found:** `CaptureCore.SnapPlayModeSafe` returns a path for a file it never wrote when `CaptureScreenshotAsTexture()` returns null (editor unfocused) — worth fixing; and `ShotController.EndExternalDrag(bool=false)` throws under reflection `Invoke(…, null)` because optional defaults are not applied, silently killing the calling coroutine. Spec + evidence: `Docs/Specs/Active/tree_occlusion_fade/`.

> **DONE (Cesar-approved) 2026-08-07 · `shot_ui_translucency_glow`** — translucency swap + tee-idle glow on the club handle. **Part A:** `ClubHandle` gets its own `CanvasGroup` (`ignoreParentGroups=true`, alpha 1) so the thing the player grabs is fully opaque while the cone sits at `ConeIdleAlpha`; new `BallConeAlphaMirror` on `CentralBall` mirrors the cone-root `CanvasGroup` alpha onto the ball `Image` each `LateUpdate`, multiplicatively on the authored RGB so it reads 0.250 at Idle and rises with the cone. `ConeAlphaController` untouched and still the **sole writer** of that group. **Part B:** new `TeeIdleGlowController` on `ClubHandle` pulses a soft gold halo after `idleGlowDelay` (5 s, unscaled) while `TurnCount==1 && ShotHistory.Count==0 && ShotState.Idle && !dragging`; `OtherButtonsFader.AnyOverlayOpen`/`mapView.IsOpen` hold the timer at 0 so it restarts on modal close; `ActionButtonWidget` wires the static `NotifyOtherInteraction()` into every action button's `onClick`; `ClubHandleDragger.OnPointerDown` calls `OnHandleTouched()` (null-safe `?.`, so bot/versus paths that never raise pointer events are unaffected). **⚠️ The real bug cost two review cycles and is the lesson worth keeping: `ClubHandle.localScale` is 2.0, and `AnimateGlow()` wrote `Vector3.one * scale` while `SyncGlowRect()` mirrored anchors/position/size/pivot but NOT scale** — so the halo rendered 194.5×109.3 px inside a 356×200 px handle, permanently occluded, while the per-frame `[GlowFrame]` log cheerfully reported `alpha=0.788`. **A log proving the component thinks it is glowing is not evidence that a pixel glowed.** Compounded by both earlier evidence sessions being captured **after a domain reload during play** — signature: raw `GAMEPLAY_STRAIGHT` localization key, `PLAYER / Lv 1`, `DRIVER 0 yrds`, non-rendering cone/ball. The reviewers correctly refused to pass those frames but could not see *why* they were broken; booting the game manually and measuring found it in minutes. **Compile first, THEN enter play, and never touch C# during a capture session.** **Cesar's two look calls, both after seeing it live:** (1) the SPEC's literal recipe — clone the handle sprite, tint gold, pulse scale — produced a hard-edged *gold echo of the club head* that also vanished entirely at the pulse trough, and the pulse grew **upward only** because `SyncGlowRect()` copied the handle's **bottom-edge pivot**; replaced with a generated 128×128 radial falloff (alpha `(1-r)²`, `preserveAspect=false` so it stretches to the wide club head), centre pivot, positioned by the handle's **world centre** via `GetWorldCorners` (which already accounts for anchoring/pivot/scale), plus `haloPadding` (serialized, 1.6) so the soft rim clears the handle on every side. Verified symmetric: glow centre `(585.0,1166.0)` == handle centre, margins left=right=107.6, top=bottom=60.5. (2) `#98855B` (the shot-UI button outline gold) was tried and rejected as **too subtle**; reverted to `#FFC94A`. **⚠️ `glowColor` is serialized on the LabScaffold component — changing the C# default alone does NOT affect the live object; the scene value must be updated too (via `SerializedObject`, never raw YAML).** Video: 1170×2532 / 30 fps / 19.0 s / 568 frames through the real boot path (Splash PLAY → Home PRACTICE → hole-selection PLAY → Lomond Hole 1), recorded by the new editor-only `TeeIdleGlowDemoRecorder` (`GOLFIN > Physics > Record Tee Idle Glow Demo`, `GameViewInputSettings` so the ScreenSpaceOverlay ShotUI survives — a camera source drops Overlay UI under URP). Verified by decoding **consecutive** frames: no glow at 3.0 s → onset at 6.0 s → real Spin `onClick` opens SpinPanel at 9.7 s → closed at 13.0 s with the countdown restarted → re-armed at 18.0 s. ⚠️ Recorder gotcha: the Home PRACTICE card's button is **also labelled "PLAY"**, so a label-only match re-clicks Home and never advances — step 3 excludes the `PlayButton` GameObject name. **⚠️ Still unverified on device:** the grab-handle-mid-glow fade (≤0.15 s) and the stroke-2/next-hole re-arm are proven at branch level and by the scripted real-widget path, but not by a human finger. Scope untouched as specced: no flick-gate/aim-lock/cone-sizing changes, no non-tee glow, no hierarchy rebuilds. Spec + evidence: `Docs/Specs/Completed/shot_ui_translucency_glow/`.

> **DONE (Cesar-approved) 2026-08-07 · `map_view_playable_area` (Order 354)** — map view now shows the playable area and nothing else. Single file (`MapViewController.cs`) + 7 EditMode tests; **zero scene edits**, `MapViewCaptureDriver` unmodified. **Diagnosis first (§3): neither branch the spec suspected fired.** The OB rect loads fine on every hole and the width-solve was not degenerate — the mess was the *design* of the Order 353b fit, which fitted the corridor width **at the ball's row only** with nothing constraining what lay beyond the tile's far edge, so everything above viewport-y ≈ 0.60 was off-tile world by construction. Compounded by `TryGetObExtent`'s `halfW` = **support function of the axis-aligned OB box** along the aim-perpendicular: on a rotated hole axis that badly over-reads the corridor (Hole 1 fitted 388 m for a ~261 m corridor; Hole 5 fitted 461 m for ~150 m), so the camera also pulled back further than needed. **Also: the reference screenshot is Hole 1, not Hole 5** — Hole 1's predicted flag/far-edge projection (0.54 / 0.60) matches the pixels; Hole 5's (0.39 / 0.43) does not. **Fix, three parts.** (1) Camera axis = **hole axis**, not live aim yaw — the aim line now rotates on screen instead of rotating the world (reference-title model); proved by a byte-identical camera pose logged across open + re-aim and a pixel-identical tile between the two frames. (2) `TryGetObExtent` + `FramePlayingAreaWidth` replaced by a **show-region** fit: `BuildShowRegionXZ` (public static, Sutherland–Hodgman clip of the OB rect by `along ≥ ballAlong − 15 m`, ≤5 verts) + `SolveShowRegionPose` (public static, generalises both 353b bisections — the two-point edge test becomes "every region vertex inside `[0.02,0.98]×[0.04,0.96]`"). The two solves are **coupled**: distance is bisected and each candidate is evaluated *after* anchoring the bottom, so containment and the K2 flush bottom both hold at the answer instead of alternating and hoping. Measured on holes 1/3/5/6 × tee+approach: all 8 contain every vertex, bottom flush at 0.040, ball below flag. **One deliberate deviation from §4.2:** anchor the region's *lowest projected vertex*, not the near-edge *midpoint* — on a rotated axis the near edge is a corner, and anchoring the midpoint would push that corner off-screen, contradicting the same sentence's goal. (3) Hard-hide the outside: `HideEnvironmentForMap`/`RestoreEnvironmentAfterMap` (own `_hiddenEnvRenderers` list — `HideShotUIChrome` clears `_hiddenBallRenderers` and runs *after* `BuildRuntimeObjects`), hole-fitted far clip, pan clamped into the OB rect, and the fallback branch's zoom-out cap fixed to `_currentFov` so **no** path can pull back past its fit (**P-008 closes inverted** — the default view IS the zoom-out stop). **Two spec premises about §4.3 were wrong and are corrected in code:** `MountainBackdrop` is a **child of `HoleRoot`**, not a scene root, so the spec's root-level scan would have hidden nothing; and the unnamed "flat bright-green plane" is **`ObGroundSkirt`** — a 9000 m `ObSkirt_Mat` plane spawned at **runtime**, in no scene file, which is why every edit-mode scan missed it. Found the way §4.3 mandates (ship without it → run the real flow → read the new permanent `off-tile renderer(s) NOT hidden` diagnostic, which now names any future offender). P-010 stays fixed-by-construction. **354b — "I want the hole to fit the frame. All holes" (Cesar, same day):** the first pass framed the **OB rect** and left ~35% of Hole 1's frame as matte. Root cause on measurement: the rect is only the mask's *bounding box*, and **Hole 1's in-bounds area is 40.3% of its rect, Hole 5's is 34.9%** — so framing the rect spent most of the screen on ground the player can never play. Three fixes: (1) the region is now the **playable footprint** — `TryGetPlayableHull` decodes `obMask.maskBase64` (bit SET = OB, per `BakedZoneClassifier.IsObAt`), samples every 8th cell, convex-hulls it (`ConvexHullXZ`, 37–46 verts), inflates 12 m so the OB line stays visible, caches per hole, falls back to the rect; (2) **aspect back-off** — clipping at `ball−15 m` leaves a stubby region near the green (80% down Hole 1: 150 m long × 167 m wide → 60% of the height wasted), so the near edge now slides back, never past the playable area's own near edge, until along/lateral hits `FrameAlongOverLateralTarget()` (2.07 on 1170×2532); (3) **bounded axis search** `_maxMapAxisTiltDeg` (25°, serialized, set 0 to disable) — a corridor running diagonally across its own rect cannot fill portrait on the ball→flag axis, so `ChooseMapAxis` sweeps ±25° scoring with `ScoreMapAxis`, **constrained to keep the ball horizontally centred (0.35–0.65)** — that constraint is what stops it rotating until the ball hugs a screen edge — with a 0.0005/deg penalty so straight holes stay straight. Result across holes 1/5/6 × tee/mid/approach: **fillX 88–99%, fillY 85–100%** (was 65%/43%/81% at the tee, as low as 34% on an approach), whole playable area still contained, bottom flush at 0.040, ball below flag in all 9. ⚠️ **Bug caught while verifying, worth knowing:** with the camera looking along `axisN` and up=+Y, `Camera.transform.right` is `(axis.z,0,−axis.x)` — the NEGATIVE of the `rightN` used in the framing maths, so any ball-x computed from `rightN` comes out mirrored (`1−x`). Symmetric extents are unaffected; screen-side reasoning is not. Regression-tested. EditMode 1010/0 (13 map tests). **354c — FINAL framing, same day: "Obground should still be green. Zoom in as much as possible as long as current ball position and flag are visible (leave a bit of margin so none of them touch the borders)".** Two changes that SIMPLIFY the task: (a) `ObGroundSkirt` removed from `_environmentHideNames` (now just `{MountainBackdrop, Backdrop, Ring}`) so off-tile ground is green again, not dark matte — and `ApplyHoleFittedFarClip` **deleted with it**, because a hole-fitted far clip would slice the very ground we now keep and put a black band at the top; the serialized `_farClip` (2000 m) is ample since at this zoom visible ground reaches only ~200 m. The oversized-survivor diagnostic went too — it would fire on `ObGroundSkirt` every open and tell the reader to hide what we deliberately keep. (b) the fit set is **the ball and the flag, nothing else** (`BuildShotRegion`, 2 points); the solve already returns the SMALLEST containing distance, so "as tight as possible" falls out — ball seats on `kShotBottomFrac` 0.08, flag on `kShotTopFrac` 0.90. Margins are screen-space on purpose: a world-space pad reads as a comfortable gap on a 460 m par 5 and swallows the frame on a 40 m pitch. Camera axis is the ball→flag axis again *for a reason*, not by reversion — any yaw off it shortens the on-screen ball→flag separation and would force the camera FURTHER BACK. That made 354b's apparatus dead weight, so it was **deleted rather than left lying around** (playable hull + `ConvexHullXZ`, `ScoreMapAxis`/`ChooseMapAxis`/`_maxMapAxisTiltDeg`, `FrameAlongOverLateralTarget`, polygon clip, aspect back-off, cache; net −108 lines vs 354b — recoverable from git if the brief changes again). One guard added: `_minFramedSpanM` (serialized, 40 m) so a 2 m tap-in doesn't drop the camera onto the deck; set 0 to disable. `SolveShowRegionPose` guard relaxed `Count<3`→`Count<2`. Measured real-flow Hole 1 tee: `dist=216.8m ballVP=(0.50,0.08) flagVP=(0.50,0.90)` — vs 407 m/(0.45,0.06)/(0.46,0.55) at 354 and 251 m/(0.64,0.07)/(0.25,0.79) at 354b. EditMode 1003/0; the 354b tests were replaced by 5 that assert the new contract (both points inside the margins at 5 lies, ball seats ON 0.08 proving tightness, distance strictly decreases as the ball advances, two-point fit set accepted, ball-below-flag swept over 12 world headings). **354d — playfield upright, same day: "The play field is still diagonally placed. I want the rectangle playfield to match the view rectangle".** Cause: **the playfield is world-axis-aligned** — the OB mask is a plain world-XZ grid (`worldOriginX/Z` + `worldSizeX/Z`, no rotation) and the terrain tile IS that grid — so pointing the camera along ball→flag rotated the tile on screen by however far the hole runs off the world axis (13.4° on Hole 1). Fix: `SnapToWorldAxis` (public static) snaps the camera yaw to the nearest ±X/±Z, applied via `SnapAxisToPlayfield` when the OB rect exists and `_alignToPlayfieldAxis` (serialized, default true) is on. On all three sampled holes the nearest axis is ALSO the field's long axis pointing at the flag, so the field stands up in portrait. **Verified geometrically, not by eye** (a pixel edge-detect just found tree canopy): projecting the OB rect's 4 corners through the real solved pose gives near/far-edge Δy **0.1480/0.0808 → 0.0000/0.0000** and side-symmetry Δ 0.5325 → 0.0258. ⚠️ The residual 0.026 is NOT rotation — it is the perspective trapezoid: at `_heroTiltDeg=70°` a ground rect projects near-edge-wider (1.30 vs 0.95 viewport units); **90° would make it a true rectangle** — a second reason the tilt field wants tuning. ⚠️ **Cost, one hole pays it:** snapping gives the ball→flag pair a lateral component the fit must absorb. Hole 5 runs **41.5°** off its field axis, so its 242 m lateral spread binds and the tee frame goes 546 m tall against a 337 m field → green ground above/below (green, not matte, since 354c). Holes 1 (13.4°) and 6 (5.9°) fill the frame 100%/100% at every lie. `_alignToPlayfieldAxis=false` restores 354c if the tighter Hole-5 frame is preferred. Real flow Hole 1 tee: `dist=210.9m ballVP=(0.76,0.08) flagVP=(0.30,0.90)` — dogleg reads as a diagonal aim line inside an upright field. EditMode 1005/0 (+2: axis choice incl. cardinals and the ≤45° bound; both points inside margins + ball-below-flag on the snapped axis across 3 holes × 3 lies incl. Hole 5's worst case). **Holes 5/6 need no separate visual sign-off for framing** — the fit depends only on the ball→flag pair and the snapped axis, both covered numerically. **⚠️ `_heroTiltDeg` is serialized `70` on the LabScaffold instance** — §4.2's 80° landed as the code default only, since §6 bans scene edits; cosmetic, the canonical screenshot at 70° already shows zero off-tile world. **⚠️ Needs Cesar on device:** pinch-to-both-stops and two-finger-pan-to-all-four-edges (no `Touchscreen` in the editor harness — the pan clamp is unit-tested and wired but the gesture path is unexercised, and it is the one path that could still reveal the outside), plus visual confirmation on holes 5/6 and green-side lies (verified numerically, but the capture driver hardcodes hole 1). EditMode 269/0. Real-flow verified through the **real** `HoleCardWidget` → `HoleMap` Button entry at 1170×2532. Spec + evidence: `Docs/Specs/Completed/map_view_playable_area/`. Absorbs and **deletes** the K2 `map_view_bottom_anchor` TellCode block.

> **DONE (Cesar-approved) 2026-08-07 · `hole_scene_leftover_v2`** — Hole_06_Geo stopped reappearing *for real*. K16 (2026-08-05) fixed the launchers but **resurrected the very leftover it was built to remove**: `CaptureSceneSetup.Capture()` snapshotted `GetSceneManagerSetup()` unfiltered, so a pre-existing Hole_06_Geo open at launch got recorded as "the user's pre-run setup" — then `Restore()` closed the staged hole scenes and immediately **re-opened it from the payload**, re-seeding the next run. A permanent cycle; 2f's defensive pre-clean couldn't break it because that sweep runs *after* `Capture()`, so it cleaned the run while the restore put the leftover back afterwards. **Fix:** a shared `IsHoleGeoScene(nameOrPath)` helper (null-safe, works on names and paths) used three ways — `Capture()` filters hole entries out of the snapshot **before** the untitled-scene check (so a hole entry is filtered, never triggering the refusal), `Restore()` skips them again as defence-in-depth against stale pre-fix payloads already sitting in SessionState, and `CloseStagedHoleScenes()` drops its duplicated `StartsWith/EndsWith` test. If filtering leaves zero entries, the key is erased and logged. Because `Restore()` sweeps staged holes unconditionally, **the first smoke run after this ships retroactively cleans the existing leftover.** Also wired `LoopV2SmokeBotMenu`, which had **never** been connected to K16 at all: `Capture`/arm at the top of `Launch()` *and* `LaunchDirectLab()`, plus a new `EnteredEditMode` restore branch gated on its own `LoopV2SmokeBotMenu.Cleanup` key — the handler fires on *every* play-mode exit, so a shared key would have made this a double-restore regression for 2e/2f/VersusHud; all four launcher key pairs verified distinct. Previously a Loop v2 run silently replaced your hierarchy with ShellScene. `LaunchDirectLab()` has zero callers (a loaded trap, and very likely how the original leftover was born — it declares `Hole06GeoPath`) and was fixed in place, not deleted. Zero `.unity` diffs, no `SaveScene` in the diff. Gates 1–2 re-driven through the **real menu items**, twice for Gate 1 — the "Excluding staged hole scene from snapshot" line appearing on run 1 **only** is what proves the cycle is broken. Spec + evidence: `Docs/Specs/Completed/hole_scene_leftover_v2/`. ⚠️ **Process finding, not a code bug:** the task's declared canonical screenshot was an information-free two-tone gradient. It cleared hook Rule 14's ≥900px resolution floor mechanically while substantiating nothing; all three review gates correctly ignored it and judged on console lines + `GetSceneManagerSetup()` dumps instead. Rule 14 checks pixel count, not whether the frame carries evidence — worth tightening for code-only tasks, where the real gate is textual anyway.

> **DONE (Cesar-approved) 2026-08-06 · `shot_ui_hidden_while_shot_in_progress`** (Quick task) — the shot-input UI is now only on screen while the shot is being set up. New `ShotInProgressUiGate` on `ShotUI_Canvas` (LabScaffold) subscribes to `ShotController.OnStateChanged`, edge-triggers on `Flicking`/`Resolving`, and restores at the `CompleteShot()` → Idle re-arm: `ActionButtons_Cluster` CanvasGroup → alpha 0 + inert; `PutterTrack`/`PuttPathRoot` hidden with their pre-shot `activeSelf` remembered (so putt mode's own toggle is never overridden); selector/spin overlays force-closed; `HoleCard` map button goes inert while the thumbnail stays fully visible (`m_DisabledColor` set to `m_NormalColor` = white, so ColorTint is a no-op) with a `ShotInProgressUiGate.ShotInProgress` guard in `HoleCardWidget.OpenMapView()`. `PowerGaugeWidget`'s show predicate `!= Idle` → `Aiming/Pulling/Timing` (it used to stay on screen for the whole flight). Cone/targeting line/club handle/central ball already hid themselves at `Resolving` — untouched; player card, hole card, wind, flag distance and settings deliberately stay up during flight (Cesar's scope call). Verified on a real Hole 1 bot playthrough: single-frame cut at launch (SPIN card RGB sum 645 → 330 at frame 59, back at 163), no flash. Spec + evidence: `Docs/Specs/Quick/Completed/shot_ui_hidden_while_shot_in_progress.md`. ⚠️ **Found in passing, NOT fixed:** `ActionButtonsRoot` on the cluster has `_shotController: {fileID: 0}` in HEAD — it has never run, so its intended "no club/ball/spin swap mid-swing" gate was never live. Left alone because wiring it changes PRE-shot interaction (`ShotController` reads raw input, so pressing the Club button also drives Idle → Aiming and could break the press-and-hold selector mid-drag). Spun off as its own task; the shot-in-progress hide does not depend on it.

> **DONE (Cesar-approved) 2026-08-06 · `SHOT_FLICK_FIX_SPEC`** — ⚠️ approved on code + EditMode evidence; **acceptance tests 1–3 (hold-and-lift ×10, normal flick fires, hitch-then-release) have NOT been run on device yet** — they are the ones that validate the fix and the ones that tell you whether `_minFlickSpeed` 1.2 is too strict. Min flick speed gate + aim lock on the upswing (`Docs/Specs/Active/SHOT_FLICK_FIX_SPEC.md`). **Bug 1 (fires on plain release):** the touch path in `ShotController.Tick` already had a velocity gate, but the **production** path is `ClubHandleDragger` → `BeginExternalDrag/SetExternalPower/EndExternalDrag`, and `EndExternalDrag` committed on *any* release with power > 0. The dragger's own check was a single-frame `e.delta.y >= 5px` — exactly what a load stutter passes. Replaced with a 6-sample ring buffer in `ShotController` (`PushTouchSample`, fed by the dragger's pointer handlers) measuring a **windowed average over `_flickSampleWindow` (0.08 s) in unscaled time**, normalized to screen-heights/sec so it is DPI-independent; a pair spanning more than `_stutterFrameThreshold` (0.1 s) is a hitch frame and is never trusted → a hitch reads LOW instead of spiking. Fail → `TransitionToIdle()`, the **existing** power-reset path (not a duplicate). **Bug 2 (aim tracks through the up-flick):** `_aimLocked` latches on cumulative upward travel from the swing's lowest point past `_reversalThreshold` (0.01 screen-heights), snapshotting `_coneFinetune` at the bottom of the swing; while latched, `SetExternalPower` and the `Tick` path both ignore new finetune, so the targeting line freezes rather than hides or snaps. Unlatched by `TransitionToIdle`, so every reset path clears it. ⚠️ **The gate is keyed to real touch samples: a driver that pushes none (VersusBot, BotDriver, Scenarios, MapViewCaptureDriver, FadeDrawWiringTests, ScreenshotHelper) is never gated** — otherwise every bot would have stopped firing. `debugDisableFlickGate` falls back to the legacy single-frame check (kept in the dragger) so the toggle restores old behavior *exactly*; `debugDisableAimLock` restores line tracking. EditMode 262/0 (9 new in `ShotControllerFlickGateTests` covering the bot bypass, the latch, jitter rejection, unlatch-on-reset, and reset-instead-of-fire). **Acceptance tests 1–5 need Cesar on device** — the velocity window depends on real per-frame timing and there is no touch input in EditMode; test 6 (both toggles → old behavior) is structurally covered but worth a device confirm. Tune `_minFlickSpeed` (default 1.2) in the Inspector if it eats real flicks; set `LogResolution` for a per-release `[FlickGate]` speed readout.
>
> **↳ FOLLOW-UP 2026-08-06 — "aiming still changes when flicking up".** The latch was firing correctly (proven by replaying a gesture through the real `ClubHandleDragger`: finetune froze at 0.305 instead of running to 0.976), and the orbit-drag camera path is already guarded by `IsExternalDragActive`, so `AimYawRadians` genuinely could not rotate. **The real defect: `_coneFinetune` was doing two jobs** — it was both the aim value AND the club handle's render position (`ShotConeView.UpdateClubHandle`: `xOffset = ConeFinetuneX × maxX`). Latching it froze the handle too, and because `maxX` is derived from LIVE `PowerNormalized` — which unwinds as the finger rises — the handle *collapsed toward the centreline* during the flick (54.3px → 12.0px, a 78% slide) while the aim sat still. That reads as the aim moving. **Fix: split the channel.** `_coneFinetune` is now LIVE (always follows the finger, exposed as `HandleFinetune`, used only to place the handle sprite); `_aimFinetune` is the AIM value (mirrors live until the upswing reversal, then frozen; exposed as the existing `ConeFinetune`, and what `CommitFlick`, `AimYawRadians` and the fade/draw bend read). Per Cesar: *"the handle should still follow the finger, it is the aiming that should not change on the UP flick."* Verified end-to-end with sideways drift during the flick — aim pinned at 0.305 while the handle tracked 0.465 → 1.000. EditMode 272/0 (11/11 in `ShotControllerFlickGateTests`). ⚠️ **Known residual, deliberately NOT changed:** `finetune = handleX / maxX` divides by a denominator that shrinks with handle height, so aim is coupled to vertical position *during the pull-back* (a constant lateral offset drifts 0.337 → 1.000 as the handle rises). The latch makes this irrelevant after the reversal, but normalizing against a constant half-base would change aim sensitivity across the pull range — a feel change that needs Cesar's sign-off before anyone "fixes" it.

> **DONE 2026-08-06 · `water_entry_presentation`** — water landings now read as an event. Splash renderers are forced to renderQueue 3100 on the material INSTANCE at spawn (the `M_Splash*.mat` assets carry 3100 but Unity resets them to 3000 every load, so the splash was drawing at the same queue as `URPWater/Standard` and coplanar with the surface → invisible). Ball sinks 0.6 m over 0.5 s then hides, instead of resting on the surface. `LoopCameraDirector` defers the OB freeze by 1.1 s **for `OBReason.Water` only** — the camera already stopped advancing via the chase clamp, so only the hard `SetTarget(null)` moved; the K10 stop-chasing ruling (2026-08-05) is unchanged for every other OB reason. Verified on a real Hole-6 water shot; camera-director EditMode tests 274/0. Spec + evidence: `Docs/Specs/Completed/water_entry_presentation/`.

> **DONE 2026-08-06 · `landing_surface_banner`** — landing-surface banner (FAIRWAY/GREEN/FRINGE/SEMI-ROUGH/ROUGH/BUNKER/WATER/OB) on ball settle; runtime clone of the 1v1 TurnBanner (Figma 4094:26052), white text, EN+JP `LANDING_*` rows. Solo + 1v1 human shots; suppressed for InCup/Tee/CartPath and for the opponent's shots. `VersusMatchController.AwaitShot()` holds AnnounceTurn until the landing banner clears (bounded 2.5s) so the two never stack. Verified through the real player path on Hole 1 + Hole 6 (ROUGH/BUNKER/FAIRWAY/GREEN/SEMI-ROUGH from real landings, WATER + boundary OB, InCup silent, JP ウォーター, 1v1 sequencing across 3 cycles). Cesar-approved; moved to `Docs/Specs/Completed/landing_surface_banner/`. One deviation he accepted: clone-only `textWrappingMode = NoWrap` so SEMI-ROUGH stays on one line inside the 534px text area.

> ### 🧹 CAPTURE LAUNCHERS NO LONGER MUTATE THE EDIT HIERARCHY — 2026-08-05 (`hole_scene_leftover` / K16, `a6b022642` + `1372da34b`)
> **Standing rule now enforced in code: a capture run leaves the editor exactly as it found it.** Launchers snapshot `GetSceneManagerSetup()` (SessionState, before any `OpenScene`) and restore it at `EnteredEditMode`, closing staged `Hole_*_Geo` scenes **without saving**. Shared helper: `Assets/Scripts/Physics/Viewer/Editor/CaptureSceneSetup.cs` — `Capture` / `Restore` / `StripSerializedHost<T>`. Handlers re-register via `[InitializeOnLoadMethod]` so a domain reload can't orphan the cleanup.
> **`SmokeRunner2eMenu` + `SmokeRunner2fMenu` now use LoopV2's "Option B":** the host GO is injected at `EnteredPlayMode` and **never serialized**, so neither launcher writes LabScaffold at all. 2f's old save-before-play was also baking 13 unrelated `_disabledAlpha` lines into the scene every run. `VersusHudCaptureMenu` got the snapshot/restore at all three staging sites. 2f's defensive pre-clean stays and now finds nothing.
> Symptom this fixes: Hole_06_Geo "kept reappearing" in the hierarchy — and worse, a leftover hole scene makes `ScanForLoadedHoleSceneAtStartup` wire the lab to the **wrong hole** on the next run that doesn't pre-clean. The committed `SmokeRunner2fHost` residue in LabScaffold is gone and cannot recur.
> ⚠️ **K16 did not hold — see `hole_scene_leftover_v2` (2026-08-07) below.** The fix *resurrected* the leftover it removed: `Capture()` snapshotted the pre-existing Hole_06_Geo as "user setup", and `Restore()` re-opened it, so every run re-seeded the next one. Fixed by filtering `Hole_NN_Geo` out of the snapshot itself.

The host falls back to "capturing current state as evidence", so **the capture looks successful while proving nothing about OB.** Don't trust `controls_2e_ob_drop.png` as OB evidence until this is fixed.
> ### ⛳ THE BALL NOW GOES IN THE HOLE — 2026-08-06 (`cup_capture_and_lipout`, `5c5e0b618` + `e8060d838`)
> **Symptom:** the ball rolled over the cup and kept going even when slow enough to drop. **Cause:** the sim was cup-blind. Cup detection was a POST-HOC scan over the finished trajectory (`BallStateMachine` → `RealCupDetector`) which by contract must not modify the path. Measured on Hole 6: a putt arriving at **0.42 m/s** — well inside the 1.5 m/s gate — was flagged `InCup` at sample 580 of 1765 while the sim rolled on for another **4.78 s past the hole**. The player watched it roll over, stop elsewhere, and only then saw the modal.
> **Fix:** decide the cup INSIDE the integrator, the only place the path can change. New `CupSpec` + `TerminationReason.CupCapture` (append-only) → `BallState.InCup`. Capture truncates and synthesizes a fall-in so the animation shows the drop. Hole 6: `BallStopped`/1765 samples/7.35 s → **`CupCapture`/615 samples/2.56 s**, ending at pin XZ and pin−79 mm. Real player path: `InCup at t+1.2s`.
> **Lip-out is speed-dependent**, driven by how far the ball sinks into the open mouth (`dip = ½g(chord/speed)²`). Two rejected models are documented with measurements: a naive reverse-and-rescale that produced a **180° wall bounce at every speed** (ratio exactly 0.700 always, so `LipRestitution` only set direction), and a linear radial blend that **stopped the ball dead on the rim** (1.945 in → 0.083 out). The shipped model reproduces the architect-locked 1.5 m/s gate rather than contradicting it — dip reaches one ball radius at ≈1.5 m/s, exactly the USGA/Penner capture speed.
> **Determinism:** `CupSpec.Disabled` reproduces the pre-cup sim sample-for-sample on raw fp — the blocking test, mirroring the Phase 6/7 gate pattern. Segment geometry is exact long-integer math on `fp.raw` (a 6 mm step's squared length is only ~2 fp LSBs, so the textbook dot/lenSq form loses almost all its precision).
> ⚠️ **Out of scope, still open:** airborne chip-ins (need entry-angle gating); no cup cavity in the green mesh, so the drop reads as the ball *disappearing* at the lip rather than visibly falling; the rim-riding "horseshoe" spin-out is not modelled (fast crossings get slowed and popped, only a few degrees of turn).
> **Tuning knobs** (design-feel, architect-tunable via `putt.csv`): `lip_restitution`, `lip_speed_damping`, `lip_pop_fraction`. `cup_depth_m` 0.10 is regulation (USGA/R&A ≥ 4 in) — not a feel value.
> **Clips are LOCAL-ONLY** (`Docs/Physics/videos/`, now gitignored per the `Docs/Specs/**/videos/` precedent — 69 MB, regenerable via `GOLFIN > Smoke > Loop v2 > Cup — …`). Full diagnosis + every measurement: `Docs/Physics/CUP_CAPTURE_STEP0_DIAGNOSIS.md`.
>
> ### 🚨 THREE UI ROOTS WERE AUTHORED INACTIVE — ALL SILENTLY DEAD — 2026-08-06 (`5c5e0b618`)
> Found while shooting the cup clips: the hole-complete modal never appeared even though `OnShotComplete: terminal=InCup` fired. `Canvas/HoleCompleteModal` was authored **inactive**, so `OnEnable` never ran and `HoleCompleteModalController` never subscribed to `GameSession.OnHoleComplete` — the whole chain fired into **zero subscribers**. Sweeping every root turned up two more of the same class:
> - **`Canvas/Toast`** → `ToastController.Instance` null forever → **every toast in the game silently never appeared** (Gacha "Coming soon", shop messages, `TOAST_COURSE_CLEARED`, tournament results).
> - **`Canvas/FadeOverlay`** → `FadeController.Instance` null → `GameplaySceneLoader` silently took its documented *"No FadeController available — fall back to an instant cut"* branch, so **screen fades never played**.
>
> Every call site null-guards, so nothing ever threw. This is PIPELINE_HARDENING trap **C2** (modal root stays active; the child panel owns visibility) — and the controllers are already written for it. The other three `Canvas` modals (`TournamentSignupModal`, `TournamentResultModal`, `VersusResultModal`) were already active; these three were the odd ones out. FadeOverlay also needed `CanvasGroup.alpha 1 → 0` — it is full-screen opaque black, so activating it as authored would have blacked out the boot screen. **Diff is 4 lines, no layout churn.** Runtime-verified: both singletons now SET, `OnHoleComplete` has 2 subscribers, boot screen renders.
> 👉 **Worth a sweep elsewhere:** any component that subscribes to a static event in `OnEnable` is dead if its root is authored inactive, and it fails silently.
>
> ### ⚙️ GREEN-TUNING TOGGLE MOVED ONTO THE SETTINGS WHEEL — 2026-08-06
> The flat-green 60×60 `ToggleButton` ("G" square) that opened `GreenTuningPanel` sat **directly on top of** the in-game settings wheel (square at -20,-20/60×60 vs `SettingsButton` at -58,-24/86×86 — near-total overlap) and is **deleted**. `GreenTuningPanel.toggleButton` is now wired to `LabRoot/ShotUI_Canvas/SettingsButton`'s `Button`, so the real wheel toggles the panel. **Scope: gameplay HUD only** — `PersistentUI/TopBar/SettingsButton` and the nav bars are untouched (verified in play mode: the TopBar button is not the wired toggle).
> `GreenTuningPanelBuilder` no longer authors the green square; it binds to the existing `SettingsButton` and hard-errors if absent, so a rebuild can't reintroduce the icon. Scene diff is surgical: 202 deletions + one changed `toggleButton` fileID, **no layout churn**. Verified through the real entry path (ShellScene → Start → mode-card Play → gameplay), wheel `onClick` toggling `panelRoot` True/False/True. The `G` hotkey still works. This is a temporary home — the wheel currently has no settings screen behind it (`SettingsButton.cs` only logs).

> 1. **Top/bottom nav bars don't reach the screen edges.** ⚠️ NOT safe-area — safe area insets top/bottom on a portrait iPhone, not left/right. Suspect a non-stretch RectTransform anchor on the bar roots, or a CanvasScaler match value against a reference resolution narrower than 19.5:9. **Check the anchors before committing to a whole "responsive review pass"** — if it's the CanvasScaler it's one value.
> 2. **Ticket icon eaten by the notch.** = safe area, exactly as Phase A2 predicted. `Assets/Scripts/UI/Core/SafeAreaFitter.cs` (`GolfinRedux.UI.Core`) was written and **deliberately left unattached** so the first smoke would show raw clipping. It did. Attach it. Trap in its own header: inset bar **content**, not bar **background**, or you get a blank strip above the top bar.
> 3. **Camera won't drag sideways during aim.** Works in-editor with a mouse → **device divergence, not a missing feature.** See the input-layer finding below.
> 4. **CentralBall sprite missing until map view opened.** ✅ **FIXED + DEVICE-VERIFIED 2026-08-03** (below).
> 5. **Map view should open bottom-anchored to the screen bottom.** Polish-backlog shaped — belongs next to P-006–P-010 as **P-011**, not as a one-off. Same `MapViewController.Open()` framing path as the open-recenter pop (P-010), so fix both in one place: compute bounds-fit framing BEFORE the first rendered frame.
> 6. **Trees don't sway on device** (wind present in editor). Suspect shader-variant stripping or the `Mobile_RPAsset` quality tier dropping the wind path — **not** a scene bug. Two suspected LOD impostors also seen on Hole 1. Feeds `9c` (Order 920) and the perf phase.
> 7. **Aiming arrow speed too fast.** ⚠️ NOT a free-form fix — that's `arrowHz`, calibrated under Order 732 and recorded as **F11** in `PHYSICS_TUNING_CHANGELOG.md`. Any change needs a new F-entry. Known open caveat: `arrowHz` **goes negative above ClubControl 60** (safe only while caps hold ≤50).
>
> ### ✅ `centralball_device_invisible` — FIXED 2026-08-03, device-verified (`1a4ad15ca`)
> Report: `Docs/Reports/2026-08-03_centralball_fix_and_compile_optimizations.md`.
> **Root cause was NOT the Architect's hypothesis.** I proposed a "null-sprite latch" (`_image.enabled = sprite != null` in `CentralBallWidget.RefreshSprite`). Code **falsified it with a discriminator instead of patching the suspected line** — correct call. Actual cause: an authored **`CanvasGroup(m_Alpha: 0)` baked onto the CentralBall GameObject in `LabScaffold.unity`** (fileID 2200000006 on GO 2200000001). Found via a render-state dump on the iOS Simulator: everything healthy except `crInherited=0.00`. The editor masked it; the iOS player rendered it faithfully invisible. `PhysicsLab_Hole1.unity` was unaffected — LabScaffold only.
> **Why the map "fixed" it:** `MapViewController` adds exactly that CanvasGroup and sets 0 on open / 1 on close, so `RestoreShotUIChrome` cleared it.
> **Fix:** LabScaffold CanvasGroup alpha 0→1 (map still manages 0/1 at runtime; `CentralBallWidget` never touches alpha, so nothing fights it) + a serialized `_defaultThumbnail` fallback for parity with `BallButtonWidget` (hardening, not root cause).
> 🔴 **NO RECURRENCE GUARD EXISTS.** `MapViewCaptureDriver.cs` / `MapViewCaptureBotMenu.cs` are **editor** tools that drive map view in edit mode. If either runs the suppression path and the scene is then saved, `alpha:0` is serialized again. This is a repeatable mechanism, not bad luck, and a **new instance of a known lesson** (editor tools mutating scene state that persists — cf. `Instantiate(_, transform)` needing `HideFlags.DontSaveInEditor`). Durable fix belongs on the capture driver: restore suppressed chrome on exit, or don't touch serialized state in edit mode.
>
> ### ✅ `hole6_tree_collision_profiles` — FIXED 2026-08-04 (`c1d38e280`), spec moved to `Completed/`
> Found while chasing smoke issue #6 (trees don't sway). Unrelated to wind, but worse: `tree_collision_profiles.csv` had **no `Fir_*` rows**, so all **434 trees on Hole 6** silently fell through `TreeObstacleLoader.GetProfile()`'s `default` fallback — 0.25 m trunk radius, 9 m canopy top, on trees that are **29–45 m tall**. Drives ball physics **and** bot trunk-avoidance (`VersusBot` → `BotTreeProbe`, `BotDriver`), so the ball under-collided and the bot under-avoided. Quiet because trunk avoidance rarely fires on straight tee→pin play.
> **Fix:** six measured rows. Measured from the LOD0 meshes of `Trees(2025)/.../BPS/Fir 0N.prefab` — the prefabs Hole 6's TerrainData actually references (confirmed by decoding the binary asset's embedded prototype GUIDs; there is a decoy copy under `Assets/Packs/`). Cross-checked against each prefab's baked `LODGroup.m_Size`, agreeing to ≤0.01 m on all six. **No re-bake** — `bake_hash` stays `a953ea8e`.
> **Scale of the correction:** canopy footprint over the 434 instances goes **10,490 → 89,700 m² (8.6×)**; trunk area 7.7×. Hole 6 plays meaningfully tighter now. If it's too punishing, `canopyRadius` is the dial (I used max foliage radius; p90 is ~35% smaller).
> ⚠️ **The other conifer rows are still unvalidated.** `tree_collisions/SPEC.md` §3a says outright the shipped numbers are *"Architect estimates — the implementer SHOULD sanity-check radii/heights against actual prefab bounds"*; nothing in that task's report shows the check happened. `MESH_01Cedar`, `Mesh_Metasequoia`, `Spruce_1` etc. are guesses. Hole 6 was just the only hole with *no* row at all.
> **Hardening landed with it:** `GetProfile()` now warns (once per distinct name) instead of falling back silently — that one line would have surfaced this the first time Hole 6 loaded. Also corrected a wrong invariant comment on `TreeObstacleProvider.CellSize`.
> 🔵 **Still open:** no bot-run video (verified deterministically at provider + full-sim level instead). The SPEC's second hardening idea — a **runtime** `bake_hash` staleness check — is **not buildable as written**: the hash only exists in the editor baker, and detecting real staleness needs the hole's terrain scene, which no player build has. The version that would catch it is an **editor-side** validator that re-harvests each hole and diffs the hash. Filed as a separate task, not done here.
